Summary

Between couch surfing and studying for the MCATs, Lena Walsh lands herself the perfect job as a live-in nanny for an adorable two year old. The only complication is that her boss Kara Danvers is single, hot, and filthy rich. When Lena is introduced to the soccer star's family, their mutual attraction results in a simple misunderstanding that forces them to pretend to be a couple.

The other issue is that they can't seem to keep their hands off of each other.

Love Language

by Sextual

She had an expert’s grasp on how to fit her belongings into small spaces, which helped in more than one situation when she found herself without a place to stay.

    For her first two years of college, couch surfing and pet-sitting kept a roof over her head during winter and summer breaks. Life in the city was expensive, and Lena struggled to afford all of her books and supplies. Her laptop was ten years younger than her and often froze in the middle of writing papers. She had a free meal plan covered by her scholarship, but in the long stretches of summer, she budgeted strictly just to eat.


    On a frequent basis, Lena walked by the job board on her campus, and in between writing papers and finishing her degree, she realized she had a talent for working with kids. She earned good money from babysitting and a lot of couples told her that she had a gift for rocking babies to sleep.


    One of her more terrible foster mothers had told her she had two career choices as an omega coming out of the system: prostitution or a low-paying job as a caretaker of pups. Lena hated that home, but she never internalized those lessons. She liked taking care of pups and respected omegas who chose to be full time mothers, teachers or nannies.


    In time, Lena finished her degree and made plans to go on to medical school. Most of her friends signed themselves up for unpaid internships or took the summer off to travel. Unlike the rest of her peers, Lena needed some kind of work while she studied for the MCAT. She also had to find a place to live after moving out of her dorm.


    One afternoon, Lena came across a job listing for a full-time, live-in nanny. The same day she submitted her application, she received a phone call from a secretary to arrange an interview.


    She took two trains the next morning to arrive at 52 Hope Street, a luxury single-family home with its own estate manager.


    Winn Schott greeted her at the door in a suit and tie. “You must be Lena Walsh. I’m Winn Schott, manager of the house.”


    Lena foolishly uttered the first thoughts that came to mind. “I didn’t know a house required management.”


    From the way Winn smiled tensely back at her, she could tell she had misspoken. Winn gestured to one of the grand parlors. “You see that door? It’s bespoke timber. Everywhere you turn in this house, there’s finery that needs to be maintained. Kara Danvers doesn’t have time for that. She doesn’t even have time to spend with her own child.”


    Lena cringed at that assessment of her new boss and meekly looked at the gleaming floor underneath her feet.


    “I don’t say that critically,” Winn continued midstride and opened the grand double doors into a spacious home office. “Kara is one of the most caring people I’ve ever met, but recent years have been a challenge for her. It’s going to take a special person to work here and look after Mia. Are you that person?”

Lena guessed she would be meeting an older alpha, possibly mid-forties – not someone who was clearly in her late twenties or early thirties. Kara’s face was like a masterpiece of sculpture—her skin clear and bronze, and her teeth in perfect rows of white. Her custom-tailored navy suit was a bit restrictive around her biceps as she moved.

The families you’ve worked for in the past have nothing but wonderful things to say. Their pups adore you.”

She sat a little higher in her chair with the need to please. “I might get flack for saying this, but I have two main ambitions in life. I want to become a doctor and a mom. I don’t come from a big family. I’d like to have one of my own someday. In the meantime, it’s nice to help others who have kids.”

    Chapter 11

    Chapter Notes

    The atmosphere at the dinner table shifted the very second Kara ended Winn’s call. She promptly stood up without looking at Lena, as if determined to leave as fast as possible.


    Alex shot onto her feet before Kara had the chance to move, and Kelly waved her hand as though magically trying to tether Kara to the spot.

    Everyone spoke over each other in a chaotic burst of emotion, yet all Lena cared about was the rigid stance of her boss, and the tight lines of frustration that tugged the corners of her mouth down.


    “I’ll go with you,” Alex stated as she wiped her mouth on a linen napkin. There was a frantic sort of energy to her movements that said she was ready to run out of the house to accompany her sister.


    Lena had to wonder how bad things had been in Kara’s previous relationship if this was the reaction Mia’s mother inspired. A genuine sense of foreboding formed in the pit of her stomach, roiling and alive as she set her cutlery aside and gracefully stood up to begin gathering their things.



    “You’ve only just arrived,” Kelly pleaded with gentle pheromones and imploring eyes, which filled Lena with a heavy kind of dread that something awful was about to happen. “Winn can–”

    “He can’t, and frankly he shouldn’t have to handle her. This is my mess and I’ll take care of it. Alone,” Kara stressed through clenched teeth that warned Alex to stay where she was before an all-out argument started between them in front of the kids. She slid her attention onto Lena and the ephemeral panic in her eyes flared into a protective glint that seemed to strengthen Kara’s resolve. She settled her hand on Lena’s upper arm with the shell of a smile gracing her lips and swallowed thickly. “Stay here with Mia and enjoy this wonderful meal for me. I’ll be back before you know it.”

    Lena had no right to argue with her boss, and yet her pheromones did it anyway.


    The sharp, astringent disagreement was better suited for a real partner than a fake girlfriend — not that Lena’s omega cared to differentiate.


    She shook her head in offense over being left behind and tried to make a case for herself that went beyond portraying a caring girlfriend.

    “This is only happening because I attended your game, isn’t it?” Lena pressed, the jut of her chin daring Kara to deny it as her emotions tried to convince her that this was all her fault.


    She couldn’t bear to think of the upheaval she might have been causing Mia just from fooling around with her boss, and a sadness took root in her heart as she thought of putting an end to it.


    “If I hadn’t piqued her curiosity, she wouldn’t be on your doorstep demanding to see you right now,” Lena argued. “It would be better if we showed up together.”

    “No, it really wouldn’t,” Kara exhaled with her whole chest and sent a look toward Alex, who immediately leapt in to agree.

    “Kara’s got this. She knows what she’s doing,” Alex intoned, though the furrow of her brow conveyed a silent you had better as she stared back at her sister.
